&=(-1)^{k}\frac{(k+1)!}{u^{k+2}}+(k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0,\ \ell+k\geq 2}^{\infty}\binom{\ell+k+1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k+2}u^{\ell}.\end{split}

(3.32)

Therefore, due to (
3.9
) and (
3.10
) we have

\displaystyle\wp^{(k)}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})-(-1)^{k}(k+1)!(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{-k-2}

\displaystyle=\wp^{(k)}\Big(\mathcal{A}(P;\infty^{j})\Big)-(-1)^{k}(k+1)!\Big(\mathcal{A}(P;\infty^{j})\Big)^{-k-2}

\displaystyle\underset{P\sim\infty^{j}}{=}(k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0,\ \ell+k\geq 2}^{\infty}\binom{k+\ell+1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k+2}\Big(\mathcal{A}(P;\infty^{j})\Big)^{\ell}

\displaystyle=(k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0,\ \ell+k\geq 2}^{\infty}\left\{\binom{k+\ell+1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k+2}\sum_{n=\ell}^{\infty}\mathcal{B}_{n,\ell}(j)\mathbf{z}_{j}(P)^{n}\right\}.

In view of this and (
3.4
), we get the following expansion for the differential
\wp^{(k)}(z_{P}-\infty^{i})\phi(P)
:

\displaystyle\quad\quad\wp^{(k)}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})\phi(P)-(-1)^{k}(k+1)!\Big(\mathcal{A}(P;\infty^{j})\Big)^{-k-2}\phi(P)

\displaystyle\underset{P\sim\infty^{j}}{=}(k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0,k+\ell\geq 2}^{\infty}\left\{\binom{k+\ell+1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k+2}\sum_{n=\ell}^{\infty}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}rx_{r}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n,\ell}(j)\right\}\mathbf{z}_{j}(P)^{n+r-1}d\mathbf{z}_{j}(P).

Thus, using (
3.12
) and recurrence relation (
2.8
) for Bell polynomials, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{j,\beta}\big[\wp^{(k)}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})\phi\big]:=\underset{\infty^{j}}{\rm res}\bigg(\lambda(P)^{\frac{\beta}{n_{j}+1}}\wp^{(k)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\phi(P)\bigg)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(k+1)!\underset{\infty^{j}}{\rm res}\bigg(\lambda(P)^{\frac{\beta}{n_{j}+1}}\Big(\mathcal{A}(P;\infty^{j})\Big)^{-k-2}\phi(P)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(1-\delta_{\beta+k,1}-\delta_{\beta+k,2}\big)(k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0,k+\ell\geq 2}^{\beta-1}\left\{\binom{k+\ell+1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k+2}\sum_{n=\ell}^{\beta-1}(\beta-n)x_{\beta-n}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n,\ell}(j)\right\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(k+1)!\beta\mathcal{R}_{\beta+k+1,k+2}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\beta+k+2}(j)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(1-\delta_{\beta+k,1}-\delta_{\beta+k,2}\big)(k+1)!\beta\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 3}^{\beta}\frac{1}{k+\ell+1}\binom{k+\ell+1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,\ell}(j)G_{k+\ell+1}.

\square

Corollary 3.2

With respect to the local parameter
\mathbf{z}_{i}(P)
defined by
\mathbf{z}_{i}(P):=\lambda(P)^{-\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}}
, the Laurent expansion of the differential
\Psi_{j,\alpha}
(
3.23
) near the pole
P=\infty^{i}
of
\lambda
is given by

\Psi_{j,\alpha}(P)\underset{P\sim\infty^{i}}{=}\bigg(\delta_{ij}\frac{\alpha}{\mathbf{z}_{i}(P)^{\alpha+1}}+\sum_{\beta=1}^{\infty}\Big(\mathcal{I}_{i,\beta}[\Psi_{j,\alpha}]\Big)\mathbf{z}_{i}(P)^{\beta-1}\bigg)d\mathbf{z}_{i}(P),

where the coefficients
\mathcal{I}_{i,\beta}[\Psi_{j,\alpha}]
are given by (
3.29
) and (
3.30
).

As an application of the results (
3.23
) and (
3.24
), we can derive the following (new) recurrence relation for partial ordinary Bell polynomials involving the rational function
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}
(
2.13
).

Proposition 3.5

For any positive integers
\alpha\leq n
, the partial ordinary Bell polynomial
\mathcal{B}_{n+1,\alpha}
satisfies

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,\alpha}&=\alpha x_{1}^{\alpha}\sum_{k=\alpha+1}^{n+1}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}\mathcal{R}_{k-\alpha,k+1},\end{split}

(3.33)

where
\mathcal{R}_{k-\alpha,k+1}=\mathcal{R}_{k-\alpha,k+1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{k-\alpha+1})
is the function defined by (
2.13
).

Proof:
Denote by
x_{r}:=x_{r}(0)
the coefficients in the Taylor series (
3.4
) of the holomorphic differential
\phi(P)=dz_{P}
near the pole
\infty^{0}
.
Then, reasoning as in the proof of (
3.12
), we get

\displaystyle{\underset{\infty^{0}}{\rm res}}\Big(\lambda(P)^{\frac{-\alpha}{n_{0}+1}}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{0}\big)^{-k-1}\phi(P)\Big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}0&\hbox{if}\quad\quad 1\leq k<\alpha;\\
\\
x_{1}^{-\alpha}&\hbox{if}\quad\quad k=\alpha\geq 1;\\
\\
-\alpha\mathcal{R}_{k-\alpha,k+1}&\hbox{if}\quad\quad k>\alpha\geq 1.\end{array}\right.

Now, due to expression (
3.23
) for the particular Abelian differential
\Psi_{0,n_{0}+1}(P)
as well as its behavior (
3.24
) near the pole
\infty^{0}
, we can write for
\alpha=1,\dots,n_{0}
the following:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle={\underset{\infty^{0}}{\rm res}}\Big(\lambda(P)^{\frac{-\alpha}{n_{0}+1}}\Psi_{0,n_{0}+1}(P)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{0}+2-k}\big){\underset{\infty^{0}}{\rm res}}\Big(\lambda(P)^{\frac{-\alpha}{n+1}}\wp^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{0}\big)\phi(P)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{0}+2-k}\big){\underset{\infty^{0}}{\rm res}}\Big(\lambda(P)^{\frac{-\alpha}{n+1}}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{0}\big)^{-k-1}\phi(P)\Big)

\displaystyle=x_{1}^{-\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,\alpha}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{0}+2-\alpha}\big)-\alpha\sum_{k=\alpha+1}^{n_{0}+1}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{0}+2-k}\big)\mathcal{R}_{k-\alpha,k+1}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{k-\alpha+1}\big),

where we used the Laurent series (
3.32
) for
\wp^{(k)}
in the third equality.

\square

3.4
Landau-Ginzburg superpotentials parametrization of the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})

This purpose of this section is to construct explicit Landau-Ginzburg (LG) superpotential of the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
in terms of the variables

\Big\{x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+1}(j):\quad j=0,\dots,m\Big\}

related to the holomorphic differential
\phi
via (
3.4
)-(
3.5
). The main result here is the following.

Theorem 3.3

Let
\lambda
be a meromorphic function on the torus
\mathbb{C}/{\mathbb{L}}
, with
\mathbb{L}
being the lattice
\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{Z}+\tau\mathbb{Z}
and
\Im\tau>0
, and
\infty^{0},\dots,\infty^{m}
be the poles of
\lambda
of order
n_{0}+1,\dots,n_{m}+1
, respectively. Let
x_{r}(j)
,
j=0,\dots,m
, be the coefficients in Taylor’s expansion (
3.4
) of the normalized holomorphic differential
\phi
near the point
\infty^{j}
. Then
\lambda
admits the following representation:

\lambda(P)=c-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right),\quad P\in\mathbb{C}/{\mathbb{L}}

(3.34)

where
c
is a constant (with respect to
z_{P}\in\mathbb{C}
),
\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j):=\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+1-k+1}(j)\big)
is the partial ordinary Bell polynomial (
2.1
)-(
2.2
)
and
\zeta
is the Weierstrass function (
2.20
).

Proof:
Using the relation
\zeta^{(k-1)}=-\wp^{(k-2)}
for all
k\geq 2
and the fact that
\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,1}(j)=x_{n_{j}+1}(j)
and taking into account equality (
3.6
), we easily see that the right hand side of (
3.34
) defines an
\mathbb{L}
-elliptic function.

Let
\Psi_{j,n_{j}+1}
be the Abelian differential of the second kind given by (
3.23
).
Then, in view of the principal part (
3.24
) of the differential
\Psi_{j,n_{j}+1}
near
\infty^{j}
and the fact that

\forall\ j=0,\dots,m,\quad\quad d\lambda(P)\underset{P\sim\infty^{j}}{=}-\frac{n_{j}+1}{\mathbf{z}_{j}(P)^{n_{j}+2}}d\mathbf{z}_{j}(P),\quad\quad\lambda(P)=\mathbf{z}_{j}(P)^{-n_{j}-1},

we deduce that the differential

\Psi(P):=d\lambda(P)+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Psi_{j,n_{j}+1}(P)

has no pole and its
a
-period is zero. Thus,
\Psi
must vanishes identically. Therefore, due to (
3.23
), we have

\displaystyle d\lambda(P)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Psi_{j,n_{j}+1}(P)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left\{2x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\zeta(1/2)-\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\wp^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right\}dz_{P}

\displaystyle=\partial_{z_{P}}\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left\{-2x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\zeta(1/2)z_{P}-\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right\}dz_{P}.

This implies that there is a constant
c
(with respect to
z_{P}
) such that

\displaystyle\lambda(P)

\displaystyle=c-2z_{P}\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right)

\displaystyle=c-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right),

where the second equality follows from (
3.6
).

\square

As a corollary, we investigate further equivalent expressions for the LG superpotential (
3.34
).

Corollary 3.3

Let
\lambda
be the meromorphic function given by (
3.34
). Then
\lambda
takes also the following forms:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lambda(P)&=c+(1-\delta_{m,0})\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{j})-\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{0})\Big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\wp^{(k-2)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right)\end{split}

(3.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=c-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\partial_{z_{P}}^{k}\log\sigma\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)\right)\end{split}

(3.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=c-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\mathbf{L}_{j,n_{j}+1}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}\big](\infty^{j}-z_{P})\end{split}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=c-\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(2x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\infty^{j}+\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}x_{k}(j)x_{n_{j}+1-k}(j)\right)\\
&\quad-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\partial_{z_{P}}^{k}\log\big(\theta_{1}(z_{P}-\infty^{i})\big)\right).\end{split}

(3.38)

Here the functions
\wp,\zeta,\sigma
and
\mathscr{K}=\log\sigma
are the Weierstrass functions (
2.19
)-(
2.21
),
\theta_{1}
is the Jacobi function (
2.34
),
\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)=\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+1-k+1}(j)\big)
is the partial ordinary Bell polynomial (
2.1
)-(
2.2
) and
\mathbf{L}_{j,n_{j}+1}^{\phi}
is the Bell operator given by (
3.7
).

Proof:
Formula (
3.35
) is a direct consequence of (
3.34
), (
3.6
) and the equality
\wp(u)=-\zeta^{\prime}(u)
. Using (
3.34
) and the fact that the function
\zeta
is the logarithmic derivative of the function
\sigma
(
2.26
), we see that (
3.36
) holds true. The expression defining the function
\mathscr{K}
and definition (
3.7
) of the Bell operators imply that formulas (
3.36
) and (
3.37
) are equal. Furthermore, relation (
2.35
) implies that

\log\big(\sigma(u)\big)=\zeta(1/2)u^{2}+\log\big(\theta_{1}(u)\big)-\log\big(\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)\big).

(3.39)

Therefore, recalling the expressions for the Bell polynomials
\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,1}(j)
and
\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,2}(j)
presented in (
2.3
), we deduce that expression (
3.38
) follows from (
3.36
), (
3.39
) and (
3.6
).

\square

Remark 3.2

Let
\lambda
be the meromorphic function (
3.34
). Then the divisor of
\lambda^{\prime}(P)
is of the form

\big(\lambda^{\prime}(P)\big)=\sum_{r=1}^{N}P_{r}-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+2).\infty^{j}=\big(d\lambda(P)\big),\quad\quad\text{with}\quad N=\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+2).

Thus, because of the LG superpotentials (
3.34
), the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
in genus one (see Section 2.3.2) can be identified with the open set of elements

\Big(c,\infty^{0},\dots,\infty^{m},x_{1}(0),\dots,x_{n_{0}+1}(0),\ \dots,x_{1}(m),\ \dots,x_{n_{m}+1}(m)\Big)\in\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{N}\times\mathbb{H}

(with
\mathbb{H}
being the upper half-plane) such that the following conditions are satisfied:

i)

\lambda^{\prime\prime}(P_{j})\neq 0
for all
j=1,\dots,N
(by the assumption of simple ramification at the ramification points
P_{j}
);

ii)

the branch points
\lambda_{j}=\lambda(P_{j})
are distinct;

iii)

\sum_{j=0}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)=0
, by (
3.6
);

iv)

the divisor
(\lambda^{\prime})
is equal to zero in the quotient
\mathbb{C}/(\mathbb{Z}+\tau\mathbb{Z})
.

Remark 3.3

It is known that the elliptic function from Theorem
3.3
can also be written as rational function in the Weierstrass sigma function (
2.21
) (or in the Jacobi
\theta_{1}
-function (
2.34
)) as follows
[
3
,
42
]

\lambda(P)=C_{1}\prod_{j=0}^{m}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{\sigma\big(z_{P}-\mathfrak{z}_{j,k}\big)}{\sigma\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j})}=C_{2}\prod_{j=0}^{m}\prod_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{\theta_{1}\big(z_{P}-\mathfrak{z}_{j,k}\big)}{\theta_{1}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j})},

(3.40)

where
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
are two nonzero constants and
\mathfrak{z}_{j,k}
are the zeros of
\lambda
. In our context, the aforementioned expressions (
3.34
)-(
3.35
) are more appropriate than the product versions in (
3.40
), as we shall see below.

The coming result presents a first useful consequence stemming from expressions (
3.34
)-(
3.35
) for
\lambda
.

Corollary 3.4

The periods of the Abelian differential
\lambda(P)dz_{P}=\lambda(P)\phi(P)
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}u:=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi(P)&=c-2\big(1-\delta_{m,0}\big)\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})\\
&\quad-\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)(n_{j}+1)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,2}(j);\end{split}

(3.41)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{u}:=\oint_{b}\lambda(P)\phi(P)&=c\tau-2\big(1-\delta_{m,0}\big)\big(\tau\zeta(1/2)-{\rm i}\pi\big)\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})\\
&\quad-\big(\tau\zeta(1/2)-{\rm i}\pi\big)\sum_{j=0}^{m}\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)(n_{j}+1)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,2}(j).\end{split}

(3.42)

Proof:
Let
\omega_{1}=1/2
and
\omega_{2}=\tau/2
be the half-period.
Then, making use of expression (
3.35
) for the meromorphic function
\lambda
and the properties (
2.25
), (
2.26
) and (
2.28
) of the Weierstrass functions, we find for
r=1,2
:

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{x+2\omega_{r}}\lambda(P)dz_{P}

\displaystyle=2\omega_{r}c+(1-\delta_{m,0})\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\int_{x}^{x+2\omega_{r}}\Big(\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{j})-\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{0})\Big)dz_{P}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)(n_{j}+1)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,2}(j)\int_{x}^{x+2\omega_{r}}\wp\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)dz_{P}

\displaystyle=2\omega_{r}c-2(1-\delta_{m,0})\zeta(\omega_{r})\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})

\displaystyle\quad-\zeta(\omega_{r})\sum_{j=0}^{m}\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}\big)(n_{j}+1)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,2}(j).

This and Legendre’s identity (
2.29
) give the desired result.

\square

Proposition 3.6

Let
j=0,\dots,m
. Then, with respect to the local parameter
z_{P}-\infty^{j}
, the Laurent expansion for the meromorphic function
\lambda
(
3.34
) near its pole
\infty^{j}
of order
n_{j}+1
is given by

\begin{split}\lambda(P)\underset{P\sim\infty^{j}}{=}(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)}{k(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{k}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{\ell},\end{split}

(3.43)

where for
\ell\geq 0
, the coefficient
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)
is defined by

\forall\ \ell\geq 0,\quad\quad\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j):=\frac{\Lambda_{j}^{(\ell)}(\infty^{j})}{\ell!}+(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1,k+\ell\geq 4}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k+\ell}\binom{k+\ell}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)G_{k+\ell}

(3.44)

and
\Lambda_{j}^{(\ell)}(\infty^{j})
is given by

\begin{split}\Lambda_{j}^{(\ell)}(\infty^{j})&=\delta_{\ell,0}c-\sum_{i=0,i\neq j}^{m}(n_{i}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\zeta^{(k+\ell-1)}\big(\infty^{j}-\infty^{i}\big)\right).\end{split}

(3.45)

Proof:
Consider the function

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j}(P)

\displaystyle:=c-\sum_{i=0,i\neq j}^{m}(n_{i}+1)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{i}\big)\right)

which is holomorphic near the point
P=\infty^{j}
.
Then, expression (
3.34
) for
\lambda(P)
and the Laurent expansions (
2.33
) and (
3.32
) of the functions
\zeta
and
\zeta^{(k-1)}=-\wp^{(k-2)}
,
k\geq 2
, near the origin, permit us to write

\displaystyle\lambda(P)=\Lambda_{j}(P)-(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\zeta^{(k-1)}\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\underset{P\sim\infty^{j}}{=}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Lambda_{j}^{(\ell)}(\infty^{j})}{\ell!}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{\ell}-(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\Bigg\{(-1)^{k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{k}}

\displaystyle-(k-1)!\sum_{\ell=0,\ell+k\geq 4}^{\infty}\binom{\ell+k-1}{\ell}G_{\ell+k}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{\ell}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)}{k(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{k}}+\Lambda_{j}(\infty^{j})+\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}-\delta_{n_{j},1}-\delta_{n_{j},2}\big)(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=4}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)G_{k}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{j}^{(\ell)}(\infty^{j})}{\ell!}+\big(1-\delta_{n_{j},0}-\delta_{n_{j},1}\big)(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1,k+\ell\geq 4}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k+\ell}\binom{k+\ell}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)G_{k+\ell}\right\}(z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{\ell}.

Hence we arrive at (
3.43
) by taking into account the convention (
3.17
).

\square

The upcoming result demonstrates that the coefficients
x_{n_{j}+2}(j)
,
x_{n_{j}+3}(j)
,
\dots
in formulas (
3.4
)-(
3.5
) can be obtained recursively as explicit function (although non trivial) of the parameters
\tau
,
c
,
x_{1}(i)
,
\dots
,
x_{n_{i}+1}(i)
,
\infty^{i}
of the Hurwitz space
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
. Note that formula (
3.46
) is useful since the coefficients
x_{n_{j}+2}(j)
,
x_{n_{j}+3}(j)
,
\dots
occur in expression (
3.30
) for the functions
\mathcal{I}_{j,\beta}[\Psi_{j,\alpha}]
which are among the required terms for calculating the desired WDVV prepotential associated with the holomorphic differential (see formula (
4.5
) below). The examples of WDVV solutions listed in Corollaries
4.1
and
4.3
illustrate the usefulness of formula (
3.46
).

Proposition 3.7

Let
x_{r}(j)
be defined by (
3.5
) and (
3.4
), with
j=0,1,\dots,m
and
r\geq 1
. Then for any positive integer
\alpha
, the following equality holds:

\begin{split}x_{n_{j}+1+\alpha}(j)&=\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+\alpha-1}\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{-n_{j}-1}{k}\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{-k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+\alpha,k+1}\big(x_{2}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+\alpha-k+1}(j)\big)\\
&\quad+\alpha\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)+\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\alpha-1}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell+1}(j),\end{split}

(3.46)

where , as above,
\mathcal{B}_{n,k}(j)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}(j)
are respectively the Bell polynomial (
2.1
)-(
2.2
)and the rational function (
2.13
), while
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}
is defined by (
3.44
).

Proof:
Due to (
3.5
) and the behavior (
3.43
) of
\lambda(P)
near
\infty^{j}
, we can write

\displaystyle(n_{j}+1+\alpha)x_{n_{j}+1+\alpha}(j)=\underset{\infty^{j}}{\rm res}\ \lambda(P)^{\frac{n_{j}+1+\alpha}{n_{j}+1}}\phi(P)

\displaystyle=(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\underset{\infty^{j}}{\rm res}\Big((z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{-k}\lambda(P)^{\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}}\phi(P)\Big)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)\underset{\infty^{j}}{\rm res}\Big((z_{P}-\infty^{j})^{\ell}\lambda(P)^{\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}}\phi(P)\Big)

\displaystyle=\alpha(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)+\alpha\sum_{\ell=0}^{\alpha-1}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell+1}(j)

\displaystyle=\alpha\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{n_{j}+1}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+n_{j},n_{j}+1}(j)+\alpha(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)+\alpha\sum_{\ell=0}^{\alpha-1}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell+1}(j),

with the third equality being a direct consequence of (
3.11
) and (
3.12
).

On the other hand, (
2.13
) implies that

\displaystyle\alpha\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{n_{j}+1}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+n_{j},n_{j}+1}(j)={\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1+\alpha}(j)

\displaystyle+\alpha\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+\alpha-1}\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{-n_{j}-1}{k}\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{-k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+\alpha,k+1}\big(x_{2}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+\alpha-k+1}(j)\big).

Thus we get

\displaystyle(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1+\alpha}(j)

\displaystyle=\alpha\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+\alpha-1}\frac{1}{k+1}\binom{-n_{j}-1}{k}\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{-k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+\alpha,k+1}\big(x_{2}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+\alpha-k+1}(j)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha(n_{j}+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)+\alpha\sum_{\ell=0}^{\alpha-1}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell+1}(j)

as desired.

\square

Example 3.1

Consider the even elliptic function defined by

\lambda(v)=c+x_{1}^{2}\wp(v|\tau),\quad\quad\text{with}\quad x_{1}\neq 0\quad\text{and}\quad c\in\mathbb{C}.

By the Laurent expansion (
2.32
), we have

\lambda(v)\underset{v\sim 0}{=}c+\frac{x_{1}^{2}}{v^{2}}+\sum_{\ell=2}^{\infty}(\ell+1)G_{\ell+2}v^{\ell}.

Therefore formulas (
3.44
) and (
3.46
) imply that
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell,0}c
for all
\ell\geq 0
and

\displaystyle x_{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\underset{0}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(v)dv=0;

\displaystyle x_{\alpha+2}:=\frac{1}{\alpha+2}\underset{0}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(v)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+1}dv=\frac{c}{2}{\alpha}x_{\alpha}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{x_{1}^{k}}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha+1,k+1}(x_{2},\dots,x_{\alpha-k+2}),\quad\quad\forall\ \alpha\geq 1.

4
WDVV prepotential associated with the normalized holomorphic differential

4.1
The main result

In this section, we look for an explicit form of the prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
of the semi-simple Frobenius manifold structure associated with the primary differential
\phi(P)=dz_{P}=\frac{1}{2{\rm i}\pi}\oint_{b}W(P,Q)
which is quasi-homogeneous of degree 0 (in the sense of Definition
2.4
).

Let
\eta(\phi)
be the Darboux-Egoroff metric (
2.49
) on the Hurwitz space
\widehat{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
induced by
\phi(P)=dz_{P}
.
From Section 2.3 (see also
[
18
]
and
[
35
,
40
]
), we already know that the following
N
functions, with
N=2+2m+\sum_{i=0}^{m}n_{i}
,
provide a system of flat coordinates of the flat metric
\eta(\phi)
:

\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}(\phi):=\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n_{i}+1}}{\alpha}\underset{\infty^{i}}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)^{\frac{\alpha}{n_{i}+1}}\phi(P),&\quad i=0,\dots,m,\quad\alpha=1,\dots,n_{i};\\
\\
\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)=\displaystyle\underset{\infty^{i}}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)\phi(P),&\quad i=1,\dots,m;\\
\\
s^{i}:=\mathbf{s}^{i}(\phi):=\displaystyle\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{i}}\phi(P)=\infty^{i}-\infty^{0},&\quad i=1,\dots,m;\\
\\
t:=\mathbf{r}(\phi):=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2{\rm{i}}\pi}\oint_{b}\phi(P)=\frac{\tau}{2{\rm i}\pi};\\
\\
u:=\mathbf{u}(\phi):=\displaystyle\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi(P).\end{array}

(4.1)

Lemma 4.1

Let
j\in\{0,\dots,m\}
,
\alpha\in\{1,\dots,n_{j}+1\}
and
x_{\alpha}(j)
be the coefficients given by (
3.5
) and appearing in the Taylor series (
3.4
) for the holomorphic differential
\phi
. Then, with the notation (
4.1
), we have

\begin{split}&x_{\alpha}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{t}^{j,\alpha}(\phi)}{\sqrt{n_{j}+1}},&\hbox{if}\quad n_{j}\geq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha=1,\dots,n_{j};\\
\\
\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)}{n_{j}+1},&\hbox{if}\quad j\neq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha=n_{j}+1;\\
\\
\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}\sum_{r=1}^{m}\mathbf{v}^{r}(\phi),&\hbox{if}\quad j=0\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha=n_{0}+1.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(4.2)

Proof:
The cases
1\leq\alpha\leq n_{j}
and
\alpha=\big(1-\delta_{j,0}\big)(n_{j}+1)
can be easily checked using (
3.5
) and (
4.1
). The expression for
x_{n_{0}+1}(0)
in (
4.2
) is nothing but (
3.6
) which follows from the residue theorem applied to the differential
\lambda(P)\phi(P)
.

\square

Relationship (
4.2
) implies that the variables

\bigg\{x_{1}(0),\dots,x_{n_{0}}(0),x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+1}(j),s^{j},\tau={2{\rm i}\pi}t,u:\quad\text{with}\quad j=1,\dots,m\bigg\}

(4.3)

serve also as a system of flat coordinates for the metric
\eta(\phi)
. Moreover, because of (
2.52
) and (
4.2
), we deduce that the nonzero entries of the constant matrix of
\eta(\phi)
with respect to coordinates (
4.3
) are given by

\begin{split}&\eta(\phi)\big(\partial_{x_{\alpha}(i)},\partial_{x_{\beta}(j)}\big)=(n_{j}+1)\delta_{ij}\delta_{\alpha+\beta,n_{j}+1};\\
&\eta(\phi)\big(\partial_{x_{n_{i}+1}(i)},\partial_{s^{j}}\big)=(n_{j}+1)\delta_{ij};\\
&\eta(\phi)\big(\partial_{\tau},\partial_{u}\big)=\frac{1}{2{\rm i}\pi},\end{split}

(4.4)

with
\alpha=1,\dots,n_{i}
,
\beta=1,\dots,n_{j}
.

For the sake of simplicity and readability, we choose to express the WDVV prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
associated with the holomorphic differential
\phi
(
3.1
) as a function of the variables listed in (
4.3
) and (
4.2
) instead of those in (
4.1
). We start with describing a preliminary expression for
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
by means of flat coordinates introduced in (
4.3
) and (
4.2
). To achieve this goal we shall apply formula (
2.56
) giving the WDVV solution induced by any primary and quasi-homogeneous differential
\omega_{0}
.

Proposition 4.1

Let
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
be the WDVV prepotential associated with the Frobenius manifold structure on the Hurwitz space
\widehat{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
and induced by the normalized holomorphic differential
\phi
(
3.1
). Let
\big\{x_{\alpha}(j):\quad j=0,\dots,m\big\}
be the coefficients appearing in the Taylor series (
3.4
),
\mathbf{s}^{i}(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}})
be the operation defined by the principal value in the family (
2.51
) and
\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]
and
\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]
be respectively the quantities defined by (
3.16
) and (
3.28
). Then
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
takes the following form:

\begin{split}&\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+u\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}+\frac{u}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(2n_{i}+2+\alpha)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{s}^{i}(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}})\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]\Big)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}(n_{i}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]\Big)\\
&+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\\
&-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}(n_{j}+1)\bigg)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j).\end{split}

(4.5)

Proof:
Since the normalized holomorphic differential
\phi
is quasi-homogeneous of degree
d_{0}=0
and by (
2.57
) the quantities
\rho_{\phi,\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{A}}}
appearing in (
2.56
) vanish, it follows that formula (
2.56
) becomes:

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi}-\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\bigg)\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A,B}\frac{d_{A^{\prime}}d_{B^{\prime}}}{1+d_{A}}\mathbf{t}^{A^{\prime}}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{B^{\prime}}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{B}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A,B}\frac{\big(1-d_{A}\big)\big(1-d_{B}\big)}{1+d_{A}}\mathbf{t}^{A^{\prime}}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{B^{\prime}}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{B}}\big)

where we have used duality relation (
2.55
) which states that
d_{A^{\prime}}=1-d_{A}
in the second equality.

Thus, observing that, due to (
2.48
),
1-d_{A}\neq 0
only when
\mathbf{t}^{A}\in\big\{\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha},\mathbf{s}^{i},\mathbf{r}\}
and taking into account notation (
4.1
) as well as the precise duality relations listed in (
2.54
), we arrive at the following expression in terms of flat coordinates given by (
4.1
):

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\phi}&=\frac{u^{2}}{2}t+u\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)s^{j}+\frac{u}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(2n_{i}+2+\alpha)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)(n_{i}+1)}\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{i,n_{i}+1-\alpha}(\phi)\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)\mathbf{s}^{i}(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}})\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)\mathbf{t}^{i,n_{i}+1-\alpha}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)(n_{j}+1)}\mathbf{t}^{i,n_{i}+1-\alpha}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{j,n_{j}+1-\beta}(\phi)\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{j,\beta}}\big)\\
&\quad+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi)-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}\bigg)\mathbf{v}^{i}(\phi)\mathbf{v}^{j}(\phi).\end{split}

(4.6)

On the other hand, because of (
2.46
), (
2.51
), (
3.16
) and (
3.28
), we have

\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{j,\beta}}(P)=\frac{\sqrt{n_{j}+1}}{\beta}\Psi_{j,\beta}(P)\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}(\omega_{0})=\frac{\sqrt{n_{i}+1}}{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\omega_{0}].

This brings us to write

\displaystyle\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)=\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}\big(\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}\big)=\frac{\sqrt{n_{i}+1}}{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}];

\displaystyle\mathbf{t}^{i,\alpha}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{j,\beta}}\big)=\frac{\sqrt{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}}{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big].

Finally, the claimed result (
4.5
) follows from (
4.6
), (
4.2
), (
4.3
) and the two previous equalities:

\square

It remains to write expressions for lines two, three and four by means of variables (
4.3
). We begin with the second line.

\bullet

Expression for the second line in (
4.5
)

The next result deals with the expression for the following functions given by the principal value

\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)=\mathbf{s}^{i}(\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}):=p.v.\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{i}}\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}},\quad\quad i,j=1,\dots,m.

Here we recall that the principal value is defined by subtracting the divergent part of the integral as a function of the local parameters
\mathbf{z}_{0}(P):=\lambda(P)^{-\frac{1}{n_{0}+1}}
and
\mathbf{z}_{j}(P):=\lambda(P)^{-\frac{1}{n_{j}+1}}
near the points
\infty^{0}
and
\infty^{j}
, respectively.

A general formula for
\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)
in terms of the Riemann theta function with (odd and non-singular) half integer characteristics was recently obtained in
[
35
]
for an arbitrary genus
g\geq 1
of the underlying Riemann surfaces. Here we prefer to rewrite the genus one case by means of the Weierstrass function
\mathscr{K}(u):=\log\sigma(u)
, with
\sigma
being the sigma function (
2.21
).

Lemma 4.2

Let
i,j=1,\dots,m
,
\mathscr{K}(u):=\log\sigma(u)
and
s^{j}:=\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{j}}\phi=\infty^{j}-\infty^{0}
, where
\phi=dz_{P}
is the holomorphic differential (
3.1
) and
\infty^{0},\dots,\infty^{m}
are the prescribed poles of a covering
\big(\mathbb{C}/{\mathbb{L}},\lambda\big)
.

i)

If
i\neq j
, then

\begin{split}\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)&=\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\mathscr{K}(s^{i})-\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+2s^{i}s^{j}\zeta(1/2)+\log\big(-x_{1}(0)\big).\end{split}

(4.7)

ii)

When
i=j
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{s}^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)&=\log\big(x_{1}(j)\big)+\log\big(-x_{1}(0)\big)-2\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+2(s^{j})^{2}\zeta(1/2).\end{split}

(4.8)

Proof:
Let
\theta_{1}(\cdot|\tau)=-\Theta_{[\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}(\cdot|\tau)
be the Jacobi function defined by (
2.34
). Using the genus one case of Theorem 3.3 in
[
35
]
, we can deduce that the functions
\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)
are explicitly expressed by means of the Jacobi
\theta_{1}
-function as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)\theta_{1}(\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})}{\theta_{1}(\infty^{i}-\infty^{0})\theta_{1}(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})}\right)+\log\big(\phi(\infty^{0})\big)-\log(-1),&\hbox{if}\ i\neq j\\
\\
\displaystyle\log\big(\phi(\infty^{j})\big)+\log\big(\phi(\infty^{0})\big)-2\log\left(\frac{\theta_{1}(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})}{\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\right)-\log(-1),&\hbox{if}\ i=j.\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, due to relation (
2.35
) which gives the link between the functions
\sigma
and
\theta_{1}
, we conclude that
\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)
can be rewritten in terms of the Weierstrass function
\mathscr{K}(u):=\log\sigma(u)
. More precisely, when
i\neq j
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{s}^{i}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\sigma(\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})}{\sigma(\infty^{i}-\infty^{0})\sigma(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})}\right)+2(\infty^{i}-\infty^{0})(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})\zeta(1/2)+\log\big(-\phi(\infty^{0})\big)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\frac{\sigma(s^{i}-s^{j})}{\sigma(s^{i})\sigma(s^{j})}\right)+2s^{i}s^{j}\zeta(1/2)+\log\big(-\phi(\infty^{0})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\mathscr{K}(s^{i})-\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+2s^{i}s^{j}\zeta(1/2)+\log\big(-x_{1}(0)\big)

and when
i=j
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{s}^{j}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)

\displaystyle=\log\big(\phi(\infty^{j})\big)+\log\big(-\phi(\infty^{0})\big)-2\log\left(\sigma(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})\right)+2(\infty^{j}-\infty^{0})^{2}\zeta(1/2)

\displaystyle=\log\big(\phi(\infty^{j})\big)+\log\big(-\phi(\infty^{0})\big)-2\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+2(s^{j})^{2}\zeta(1/2)

\displaystyle=\log\big(x_{1}(j)\big)+\log\big(-x_{1}(0)\big)-2\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+2(s^{j})^{2}\zeta(1/2).

This establishes the desired formulas (
4.7
) and (
4.8
).

\square

As a direct consequence of formulas (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) and the following link between
\zeta(1/2|\tau)
and the quasi-modular Eisenstein series
G_{2}(\tau)
:

\zeta(1/2|\tau)=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau),\quad\quad\quad\forall\ \tau,\quad\Im\tau>0,

(4.9)

we obtain the expression for the second line of the prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
(
4.5
). Relation (
4.9
) can be found in
[
3
]
(Section 20) and
[
18
]
(formulas (C.72)-(C.73)) and can be seen from the Fourier expansion (
2.18
) for the Eisenstein series
G_{2}
.

Proposition 4.2

Let
\mathscr{K}(u)=\log\sigma(u)
and
G_{2}
be the quasi-modular Eisenstein series defined by (
2.16
) and (
2.18
). Then the second line in expression (
4.5
) for the WDVV prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
is given by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{s}^{i}(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j})

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}(n_{i}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathscr{K}(s^{j})\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big((n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big)^{2}\log\big(x_{1}(j)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg)^{2}\log\big(x_{1}(0)\big)+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg)^{2}.

\bullet

Expression for the third line in (
4.5
)

For
0\leq i\leq m
,
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq\alpha\leq n_{i}
, let us introduce the following quantities

\begin{split}&\varrho(i,j,\alpha):=\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]-2{\alpha}x_{\alpha}(i)s^{j}\zeta(1/2);\\
&A_{ij}:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(i,j,\alpha)\Big);\\
&B_{ij}:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(i,j,\alpha)\Big).\end{split}

(4.10)

Then in terms of
\varrho(i,j,\alpha)
,
A_{ij}
and
B_{ij}
, we can write the third line as follows:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]\Big)

\displaystyle=\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(i,j,\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle=\zeta(1/2)\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}A_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}B_{ij}.

Therefore, it suffices to express the functions
A_{ij}
and
B_{ij}
in terms of flat coordinates (
4.2
)-(
4.3
). We distinguish three cases.

First case:
i\neq j
.
Because of (
3.18
) we have

\displaystyle A_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}[\mathscr{K}](s^{i}-s^{j})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}[\mathscr{K}](s^{i}).

Moreover, (
3.18
) and recurrence relation (
2.8
) for partial ordinary Bell polynomials imply that

\displaystyle B_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,k}(i)\Big(\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(s^{i})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(k+1)!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k+1}(i)\Big(\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(s^{i})\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=2}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\Big(\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(s^{i})\Big).

Second case:
i=j\geq 1
.
Expressions (
3.19
) and (
4.10
) for
\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]

and
\varrho(i,j,\alpha)
imply that

\displaystyle A_{jj}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(j,j,\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=4}^{\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)}{\ell}G_{\ell}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}\big](s^{j})

and

\displaystyle B_{jj}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(j,j,\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=4}^{\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)}{\ell}G_{\ell}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,k}(j)\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(s^{j})\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\ell=4}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{\ell(\ell+1)}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,\ell+1}(j)G_{\ell}-\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(s^{j}),

where, as in the first case, the last equality follows from recurrence relation (
2.8
) for Bell polynomials.

Third case:
i=0
and
j\geq 1
.
As above, using now (
3.20
) we have

\displaystyle A_{0j}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{0}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(0,j,\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle=-(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)}{n_{0}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(0)

\displaystyle\quad+(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\Bigg\{\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)}{n_{0}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=4}^{\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(0)}{\ell}G_{\ell}\bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)}{n_{0}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\mathbf{L}_{0,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}\big](-s^{j})

and

\displaystyle B_{0j}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\varrho(0,j,\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(0)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=4}^{\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(0)}{\ell}G_{\ell}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,k}(0)\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(-s^{j})\bigg)

\displaystyle=-(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(0)-\sum_{\ell=4}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{\ell(\ell+1)}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,\ell+1}(0)G_{\ell}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{k=2}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(-s^{j})

\displaystyle=-(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}(n_{0}+1-\alpha)x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(0)-\sum_{\ell=4}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{\ell(\ell+1)}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,\ell+1}(0)G_{\ell}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(-s^{j})-(n_{0}+1)x_{n_{0}+1}(0)\mathscr{K}(s^{j})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\log(-1)\big)(n_{0}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{0}+1}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j).

Here, in the last equality we have taken into account the fact that

\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,1}(0)=x_{n_{0}+1}(0)\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\mathscr{K}(-u)=\log\sigma(-u)=\log(-1)+\mathscr{K}(u).

Summarizing the above computations and bearing in mind the relation
G_{2}(\tau)=2\zeta(1/2|\tau)
, we arrive at the following explicit form for the third line.

Proposition 4.3

Using notation of Theorem
3.1
, the third line in (
4.5
) has the following form in terms of flat coordinates listed in (
4.3
) and (
4.2
):

\displaystyle\Sigma_{3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left(\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}+\frac{n_{i}+1-\alpha}{n_{i}+1}\right)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}[\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}]\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(2n_{i}+2+\alpha)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}(n_{j}+1)x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}[\mathscr{K}](s^{i}-s^{j})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}(n_{0}+1)x_{n_{0}+1}(0)\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}[\mathscr{K}](s^{i})\Bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=2}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(s^{i}-s^{j})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=2}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(s^{i})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{n_{j}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=4}^{\alpha}\frac{\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)}{\ell}G_{\ell}\bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)-\sum_{\ell=4}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{\ell(\ell+1)}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,\ell+1}(j)G_{\ell}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{0}+1+\alpha}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathbf{L}_{0,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}\big](-s^{j})\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\{(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(-s^{j})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}(n_{0}+1)x_{n_{0}+1}(0)\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathscr{K}(s^{j})+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg)^{2},

where, by (
3.6
), we have

(n_{0}+1)x_{n_{0}+1}(0)=-\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j).

\bullet

Expression for the fourth line in (
4.5
)

Lastly, we move on to find the explicit form of the fourth line involving the functions
\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]
(
3.28
):

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}(n_{i}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}(n_{i}+1)x_{\alpha}(i)x_{\beta}(j)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\chi(i,j;\alpha,\beta),

where

\chi(i,j;\alpha,\beta):=\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}\Big(\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]-2\alpha\beta x_{\alpha}(i)x_{\beta}(j)\zeta(1/2)\Big).

(4.11)

Lemma 4.3

For
i,j=0,\dots,m
, define the functions

\mathbf{Q}_{ij}:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\chi(i,j;\alpha,\beta),

(4.12)

where
\chi(i,j;\alpha,\beta)
is given by (
4.11
).

1)

If
i\neq j
, then

\begin{split}\mathbf{Q}_{ij}&=-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\Bigg\{\left(\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\right)\\
&\quad\quad\times\bigg(\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big](\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})\bigg)\Bigg\}.\end{split}

(4.13)

2)

When
i=j
we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{Q}_{jj}&=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n_{j}+1}\Bigg\{\left[(-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\right]\\
&\quad\quad\times\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k+\ell-1}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)G_{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\},\end{split}

(4.14)

where
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\alpha+k}(j)\big)
denotes the rational function (
2.13
).

Proof:
Assume first that
i\neq j
. Then expression (
3.29
) for
\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]
and (
4.11
) imply that

\displaystyle\chi(i,j;\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\alpha}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!\ell!}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(i)\wp^{(k+l-2)}(\infty^{i}-\infty^{j}).

Now, using (
4.12
) and applying recurrence relation (
2.8
) for partial ordinary Bell polynomials and observing that
\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(u)=-\wp(u)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\left(\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\alpha}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!\ell!}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(i)\wp^{(k+l-2)}(\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\left((-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)(k+1)!}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k+1}(j)\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{\ell!}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(i)\wp^{(k+l-2)}(\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\left((-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)k!}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big](\infty^{i}-\infty^{j})\right).

Let us now deal with the case where
i=j
. As above, by employing (
3.30
) and (
4.11
) we deduce

\displaystyle\chi(j,j;\alpha,\beta):=\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}\Big(\mathcal{I}_{j,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]-2\alpha\beta x_{\alpha}(j)x_{\beta}(j)\zeta(1/2)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k,k+1}(j)-\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\bigg((-1)^{k}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 4}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k+\ell}\binom{k+\ell}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)G_{k+\ell}\bigg).

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{jj}:=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\chi(j,j;\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k,k+1}(j)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\Bigg\{\left(\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\beta}\bigg[(-1)^{k}\mathcal{B}_{\beta,k}(j)\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 4}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k+\ell}\binom{k+\ell}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)G_{k+\ell}\bigg]\right]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n_{j}+1}\Bigg\{\left((-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{k+\ell-1}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)G_{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\}.

\square

Proposition 4.4

Let
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\alpha+k}(j)\big)
be the rational function (
2.13
). Then with the notation of Theorem
3.2
, the expression for the fourth line in (
4.5
) is given by

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}(n_{i}+1)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{I}_{i,\alpha}\big[\Psi_{j,\beta}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{\beta}(j)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left\{\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\bigg(\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big](s^{i}-s^{j})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left\{\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{0}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\bigg(\sum_{k=2}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big](s^{i})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\left\{\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{0}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\bigg(\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{0,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big](-s^{j})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=2,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n_{j}+1}\Bigg\{\left[(-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)\frac{G_{k+\ell-1}}{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\}.

Proof:
Since

\infty^{i}-\infty^{j}=\delta_{j,0}s^{i}-\delta_{i,0}s^{j}+(1-\delta_{i,0}-\delta_{j,0})(s^{i}-s^{j}),\quad\quad\forall\ i,j=0,\dots,m,

and

\displaystyle\Sigma_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{\beta}(j)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\mathbf{Q}_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbf{Q}_{i0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{Q}_{0j}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathbf{Q}_{jj},

it follows that the desired result holds by employing expressions (
4.13
) and (
4.14
).

\square

We are in position to finally state our main theorem which is an immediate consequence of (
4.5
), Propositions
4.2
,
4.3
and
4.4
and simples computations.

Theorem 4.1

Consider the semi-simple Frobenius-Hurwitz manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
induced by the normalized holomorphic differential
\phi(P)=dz_{P}
and denote by
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
the corresponding WDVV prepotential.
Let

1.

\mathscr{K}
be the function
\mathscr{K}(v)=\mathscr{K}(v|\tau):=\log\sigma(v|\tau)
, with
\sigma
being the Weierstrass sigma function and
G_{2\ell}
be the Eisenstein series (
2.18
);

2.

\Big\{x_{\alpha}(j):\quad\text{with}\quad j=0,\dots,m,\ \alpha=1,\dots,n_{j}+1\Big\}
be the variables defined by (
3.4
)-(
3.5
) and satisfying (
3.6
) and
\tau,u,s^{j}
be such that

\tau:=\oint_{b}\phi(P),\quad\quad u:=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi(P)\quad\quad\text{and}\quad s^{j}:=\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{j}}\phi(P)=\infty^{j}-\infty^{0};

3.

\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)=\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+2-k}(j)\big)
be the partial ordinary Bell polynomials (
2.1
)- (
2.2
);

4.

\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi}
be the Bell operator defined by (
3.7
);

5.

\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\alpha+k}(j)\big)
be the rational function given by (
2.13
) which depends (when
\alpha+k>n_{j}+1)
on the quantities
x_{n_{j}+1+\beta}(j)
,
\beta=1,\dots,n_{j}
, determined by formula (
3.46
).

Then, with the above notation, the WDVV prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+u\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}+\frac{u}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(2n_{i}+2+\alpha)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)s^{j}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(2n_{i}+2+\alpha)}{n_{i}+1+\alpha}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1-\beta)(n_{i}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}(i)x_{\beta}(j)x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)x_{n_{j}+1-\beta}(j)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big(s^{i}-s^{j}|\tau\big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{k=1}^{n_{i}+1}\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{k!}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}+1,k}(i)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big(s^{i}|\tau\big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\{(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(n_{0}+1)}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}\big(-s^{j}|\tau\big)\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left\{\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(\cdot|\tau)\big](s^{i}-s^{j})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{i}}\left\{\frac{(n_{i}+1-\alpha)(n_{i}+1)(n_{0}+1)}{(n_{i}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{i}+1-\alpha}(i)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{0}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{0}+1,k}(0)\mathbf{L}_{i,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(\cdot|\tau)\big](s^{i})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{0}}\left\{\frac{(n_{0}+1-\alpha)(n_{0}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{(n_{0}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{0}+1-\alpha}(0)\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathbf{L}_{0,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k-1)}(\cdot|\tau)\big](-s^{j})\bigg)\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}+1}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\sum_{k=1,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n_{j}+1}\Bigg\{\left[(-1)^{k}\frac{(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{k(n_{j}+1+\alpha)}x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}(j)\frac{G_{k+\ell-1}(\tau)}{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Bigg\{\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}(n_{j}+1-\alpha)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}(j)-\sum_{\ell=4}^{n_{j}}\frac{(n_{j}+1)^{2}}{\ell(\ell+1)}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\mathcal{B}_{n_{j}+1,\ell+1}(j)G_{\ell}(\tau)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big((n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\Big)^{2}\log\big(x_{1}(j)\big)+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg)^{2}\log\big(x_{1}(0)\big)

\displaystyle+\log(-1)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\bigg)^{2}+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j)

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\bigg(\frac{(n_{i}+1)(n_{j}+1)}{n_{0}+1}+\delta_{ij}(n_{j}+1)\bigg)x_{n_{i}+1}(i)x_{n_{j}+1}(j).

Let us point out that, by (
2.59
) and (
4.2
), the unit and Euler vector fields of the
\phi
-Frobenius manifold structure on
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
are such that

e=\partial_{u}
and

E=u\partial_{u}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}x_{n_{j}+1}(j)\partial_{x_{n_{j}+1}(j)}+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}x_{\alpha}(j)\partial_{x_{\alpha}(j)}.

(4.15)

In addition, the prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
form Theorem
4.1
is a quasi-homogeneous function of degree
2
with respect to the Euler vector field (
4.15
):

E.\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=2\mathbf{F}_{\phi}

and solves the WDVV equations (
1.1
) with respect to the
N=2+2m+\sum_{k=0}^{m}n_{k}
variables

u,\tau,s^{1},\dots,s^{m},x_{1}(0),\dots,x_{n_{0}}(0),x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{n_{j}+1}(j),\quad\quad\text{with}\quad j=1,\dots,m

where the distinguished variable corresponding to
F
is
u
, i.e. the constant Gram matrix (
4.4
) of the flat metric
\eta(\phi)
coincides with the Hessian matrix of
\partial_{u}\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
.

On the other hand, as we have already highlighted, a crucial aspect in constructing of the explicit form of the specific WDVV prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
relies on the fact that the procedure requires only the computation of some flat coordinates among the list
\big\{\mathbf{t}^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{B}}\big)\big\}
related to the
N
primary differentials listed in (
2.46
). In fact, the remaining operations
\big\{\mathbf{t}^{A}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{B}}\big)\big\}
, whose calculations are in general rather difficult, can be directly obtained from the Hessian matrix of the function
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
using formula (
2.58
). For example, equality (
2.58
), duality relations (
2.54
) and (
4.2
) lead to the following results:

\begin{split}&\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}(P)=\mathbf{u}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{s}^{j}}\big)=\partial_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}(\phi)}\partial_{{\mathbf{s}^{j}}^{\prime}(\phi)}\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=\frac{2{\rm i}\pi}{n_{j}+1}\partial_{\tau}\partial_{x_{n_{j}+1}(j)}\mathbf{F}_{\phi};\\
&\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\Psi_{j,n_{j}+1}(P)=\mathbf{u}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{v}^{j}}\big)=\partial_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}(\phi)}\partial_{{\mathbf{v}^{j}}^{\prime}(\phi)}\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=2{\rm i}\pi\partial_{\tau}\partial_{s^{j}}\mathbf{F}_{\phi};\\
&\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\Psi_{j,\alpha}(P)=\frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{n_{j}+1}}\mathbf{u}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{t}^{j,\alpha}}\big)=\frac{2{\rm i}\pi\alpha}{n_{j}+1}\partial_{\tau}\partial_{x_{n_{j}+1-\alpha}(j)}\mathbf{F}_{\phi};\\
&\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\bigg(\oint_{a}\lambda(Q)W(P,Q)\bigg)=\mathbf{u}\big(\phi_{\mathbf{u}}\big)=\big(2{\rm i}\pi\big)^{2}\partial_{\tau}^{2}\mathbf{F}_{\phi},\end{split}

(4.16)

where
\lambda
is the meromorphic function given by (
3.35
),
\Omega_{\infty^{0}\infty^{j}}
is the Abelian differential of the third d given by (
3.14
),
\Psi_{j,\alpha}
is the Abelian differentials of the second kind (
3.23
),
W(P,Q)
is the genus one bidifferential (
2.42
) and
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
is given by Theorem
4.1
.

4.2
Some special cases

By considering particular choices of the assigned combinatorial parameters
(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
of the Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n_{0},\dots,n_{m})
associated with
\phi
, this section is devoted to discuss and highlight some interesting specific cases of the WDVV solution
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
presented
in Theorem
4.1
.

To accomplish this goal, we need the following special cases of the rational function
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\mu+1})
(
2.13
).

Lemma 4.4

Let
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}
be the rational function given by (
2.13
). Then we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{1,1}&=-\frac{x_{2}}{x_{1}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{2,1}&=\frac{x_{3}}{x_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{x_{1}^{2}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{2,2}&=\frac{x_{3}}{x_{1}^{2}}-\frac{x_{2}^{2}}{x_{1}^{3}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{3,1}&=\frac{x_{4}}{x_{1}}-\frac{x_{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\frac{x_{2}^{3}}{x_{1}^{3}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{3,2}&=\frac{x_{4}}{x_{1}^{2}}-2\frac{x_{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{3}}+\frac{x_{2}^{3}}{x_{1}^{4}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{3,3}&=\frac{x_{4}}{x_{1}^{3}}-3\frac{x_{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{4}}+2\frac{x_{2}^{3}}{x_{1}^{5}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{4,2}&=\frac{x_{5}}{x_{1}^{2}}-\frac{2x_{2}x_{4}+x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{3}}+\frac{3x_{2}^{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{4}}-\frac{x_{2}^{4}}{x_{1}^{5}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{4,3}&=\frac{x_{5}}{x_{1}^{3}}-3\frac{x_{2}x_{4}}{x_{1}^{4}}-\frac{3}{2}\frac{x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{4}}+6\frac{x_{2}^{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{5}}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{x_{2}^{4}}{x_{1}^{6}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{4,4}&=\frac{x_{5}}{x_{1}^{4}}-2\frac{2x_{2}x_{4}+x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{5}}+10\frac{x_{2}^{2}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{6}}-5\frac{x_{2}^{4}}{x_{1}^{7}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{5,3}&=\frac{x_{6}}{x_{1}^{3}}-3\frac{x_{2}x_{5}+x_{3}x_{4}}{x_{1}^{4}}+6\frac{x_{2}^{2}x_{4}+x_{2}x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{5}}-10\frac{x_{2}^{3}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{6}}+3\frac{x_{2}^{5}}{x_{1}^{7}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{5,4}&=\frac{x_{6}}{x_{1}^{4}}-4\frac{x_{2}x_{5}+x_{3}x_{4}}{x_{1}^{5}}+10\frac{x_{2}^{2}x_{4}+x_{2}x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{6}}-20\frac{x_{2}^{3}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{7}}+7\frac{x_{2}^{5}}{x_{1}^{8}};\\
\mathcal{R}_{6,4}&=\frac{x_{7}}{x_{1}^{4}}-2\frac{2x_{2}x_{6}+2x_{3}x_{5}+x_{4}^{2}}{x_{1}^{5}}+\frac{10}{3}\frac{3x_{2}^{2}x_{5}+6x_{2}x_{3}x_{4}+x_{3}^{3}}{x_{1}^{6}}-10\frac{2x_{2}^{3}x_{4}+3x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}}{x_{1}^{7}}\\
&\quad+35\frac{x_{2}^{4}x_{3}}{x_{1}^{8}}-\frac{28}{3}\frac{x_{2}^{6}}{x_{1}^{9}}.\end{split}

(4.17)

Proof:
These expressions are gotten by straightforward application of definition (
2.13
) using relations (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) for partial Bell polynomials.

\square

\bullet

The Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(0,\dots,0)

We first start with the Hurwitz space of
\mathbb{L}
-elliptic functions
\lambda
having simple poles
\infty^{0},\dots,\infty^{m}
. That is, by (
3.35
),
\lambda
is given by

\lambda(P)=c+\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\Big(\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{j})-\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{0})\Big),\quad\quad m\geq 1.

In this case, the Frobenius manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(0,\dots,0)
is of dimension
N=2+2m
and flat coordinates (
4.3
) of the metric
\eta(\phi)
(
2.49
) are
u=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi(P)
,
\tau=\oint_{b}\phi(P)
and

x^{j}=x_{1}(j)=\underset{\infty^{j}}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)\phi(P),\quad\quad s^{j}=\infty^{j}-\infty^{0},\quad\quad j=1,\dots,m.

Proposition 4.5

Assume that
n_{0}=\dots=n_{m}=0
. Then, with the preceding notation, the WDVV prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
of the semi-simple Frobenius-Hurwitz manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(0,\dots,0)
takes the following form

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\phi}&=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+u\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}s^{j}+\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}s^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}x^{i}x^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j}|\tau)\\
&\quad-\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}x^{i}\bigg)\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{j}|\tau)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\log(x^{j})+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\bigg)^{2}\log\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}x^{i}\bigg)\\
&\quad+\log(-1)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{\log(-1)}{4}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}x^{i}x^{j}-\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{ij}\big)x^{i}x^{j},\end{split}

(4.18)

where
\mathscr{K}(v|\tau)=\log\sigma(v|\tau)
and
\sigma
is the Weierstrass function (
2.21
). Moreover, the unit and Euler vector fields (
4.15
are

e=\partial_{u},\quad\quad E=u\partial_{u}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}\partial_{x^{j}}.

Proof:
The result follows from Theorem
4.1
and the relations

\mathscr{K}(-v)=\log(-1)+\mathscr{K}(v),\quad\quad x^{0}=-\big(x^{1}+\dots+x^{m}\big).

\square

Remark 4.1

The WDVV solution (
4.18
) was obtained in
[
35
]
and written in terms of the Jacobi
\theta_{1}
-function. The case
m=1
was first worked out in
[
20
,
15
]
using the Dubrovin bilinear method.

\bullet

The Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(1,\dots,1)

Let us now deal with the WDVV solution
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
associated with the Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold parameterized by elliptic functions of the following form:

\displaystyle\lambda(P)

\displaystyle=c+2(1-\delta_{m,0})\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\Big(\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{j})-\zeta(z_{P}-\infty^{0})\Big)+\sum_{j=0}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\wp\big(z_{P}-\infty^{j}\big).

This is nothing but the case where
n_{j}=1
and

\begin{split}&x_{2}(j):=\frac{1}{2}\underset{\infty^{j}}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)\phi(P)=t^{j};\\
&x_{1}(j):=\underset{\infty^{j}}{{\rm res}\ }\sqrt{\lambda(P)}\phi(P)=x^{j},\end{split}\quad\quad\quad\forall\ j=0,\dots,m

(4.19)

of formula (
3.35
). The list of flat coordinates (
4.3
) reduces to

\Big\{u,\tau,s^{1}=\infty^{1}-\infty^{0},\dots,s^{m}=\infty^{m}-\infty^{0},t^{1},\dots,t^{m},x^{0},x^{1},\dots,x^{m}\Big\}.

(4.20)

Proposition 4.6

Let
m\geq 0
. Then the prepotential of the
(3m+3)
-dimensional semi-simple Frobenius-Hurwitz manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(1,\dots,1)
is the function of variables (
4.20
) given by

\begin{split}&\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+2u\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}+u\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}+2G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}+\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\bigg)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\bigg)^{2}+2\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}t^{i}t^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j}|\tau)+2\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j}|\tau)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i}x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j}|\tau)-4\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{j}|\tau)\bigg)-2\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j}|\tau)\bigg)\\
&-2(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j}|\tau)-(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{j}|\tau)+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}(t^{j})^{2}\log(x^{j})+2\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\bigg)^{2}\log(x^{0})\\
&+2\log(-1)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\log(-1)\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}t^{i}t^{j}-3\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)^{2}-4\sum_{j=1}^{m}(t^{j})^{2}.\end{split}

(4.21)

The function
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
satisfies the WDVV equations (
1.1
), where the distinguished variable corresponding to the constant invertible matrix is
u
and
\partial_{u}
plays the role of the unit vector field. Furthermore, we have
E.\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=2\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
, with
E
being the following Euler vector field

E=u\partial_{u}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\partial_{t^{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m}x^{j}\partial_{x^{j}}.

Proof:
Using Theorem
4.1
and the fact that all the poles of the coverings
\lambda
are double and taking into account notation (
4.19
) as well as expressions for the functions
\mathcal{R}_{1,1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{2,2}
listed in (
4.17
), we can see that the expression for the prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{\phi}=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+2u\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}+\frac{5}{6}u\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}+2G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{5}{3}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}s^{j}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{i,j=0}^{m}\big(x^{i}x^{j}\big)^{2}+2\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}t^{i}t^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{i}-s^{j})+\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j})-2\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}t^{i}t^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{i})

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i})-2\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\mathscr{K}(s^{j})\bigg)-(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j})+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i}x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j})-\frac{2}{3}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i})\bigg)-\frac{2}{3}(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{j})-\frac{2}{3}(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}x_{3}(j)+\frac{1}{3}x^{0}x_{3}(0)+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}(t^{j})^{2}\log(x^{j})+2\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\bigg)^{2}\log(x^{0})

\displaystyle+2\log(-1)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}t^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\log(-1)\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}t^{i}t^{j}-\frac{7}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\big(1+\delta_{ij}\big)t^{i}t^{j}.

On the other hand, because of (
3.46
) we have

\displaystyle x_{3}(j)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{-1}\big(x_{2}(j)\big)^{2}+x_{2}(j)\mathcal{R}_{1,1}(j)+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(j)\mathbf{f}_{0}(j)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\big(x_{1}(j)\big)^{-1}\big(x_{2}(j)\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(j)c+x_{1}(j)\sum_{i=0,i\neq j}^{m}x_{2}(i)\zeta(\infty^{j}-\infty^{i})-\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(j)\sum_{i=0,i\neq j}^{m}\big(x_{1}(i)\big)^{2}\zeta^{\prime}(\infty^{j}-\infty^{i})

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\frac{(t^{j})^{2}}{x^{j}}+\frac{1}{2}x^{j}\left(u+4\zeta(1/2)\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}s^{i}+2\zeta(1/2)\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\right)+x^{j}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{m}t^{i}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j}-s^{i})

\displaystyle\quad-x^{j}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j})-\frac{1}{2}x^{j}\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{j}-s^{i})-\frac{1}{2}x^{j}(x^{0})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{j}),

with
j=1,\dots,m
and

\displaystyle x_{3}(0)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\big(x_{1}(0)\big)^{-1}\big(x_{2}(0)\big)^{2}+x_{2}(0)\mathcal{R}_{1,1}(0)+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(0)\mathbf{f}_{0}(0)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\big(x_{1}(0)\big)^{-1}\big(x_{2}(0)\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(0)c+x_{1}(0)\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{2}(i)\zeta(\infty^{0}-\infty^{i})-\frac{1}{2}x_{1}(0)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big(x_{1}(i)\big)^{2}\zeta^{\prime}(\infty^{0}-\infty^{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2x^{0}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{x^{0}}{2}\left(u+4\zeta(1/2)\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}s^{i}+2\zeta(1/2)\sum_{i=0}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\right)-x^{0}\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i})-\frac{1}{2}x^{0}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(x^{i})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{i}).

Therefore, from equality (
4.9
) relating
\zeta(1/2)
and the Eisenstein series
G_{2}
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\sum_{j=1}^{m}x^{j}x_{3}(j)+\frac{1}{3}x^{0}x_{3}(0)

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{6}\sum_{j=0}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}+\frac{1}{3}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}s^{i}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}G_{2}(\tau)\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}t^{i}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{3}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{j})-\frac{1}{3}(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\mathscr{K}^{\prime}(s^{i})-\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i,j=1,i\neq j}^{m}(x^{i}x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{i}-s^{j})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{3}(x^{0})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{j})^{2}\mathscr{K}^{\prime\prime}(s^{j})+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{i}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(t^{j})^{2}.

Substituting this into the fifth line of the above expression for
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
, we arrive at the desired explicit form (
4.21
).

\square

\bullet

The Hurwitz-Frobenius manifold
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n)
, with
n\geq 1
.

Let us assume that the Hurwitz space is of type
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n)
, with
n\geq 1
. Let
[(\mathbb{C}/{\mathbb{L}},\lambda)]
be a point of
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n)
.
This means that the meromorphic function
\lambda
has a unique pole
\infty^{0}
of order
n+1\geq 2
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
z_{\infty^{0}}=0
.
Thus, the LG superpotential formula (
3.35
) and expression (
3.41
) for
u:=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi(P)
imply that
\lambda
is given by

\begin{split}\lambda(P)&=c+(n+1)\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n-k+2})\wp^{(k-2)}\big(z_{P}\big)\\
&=u+\frac{n+1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{k=1}^{n}x_{k}x_{n+1-k}\\
&\quad+(n+1)\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n-k+2})\wp^{(k-2)}\big(z_{P}\big),\end{split}

(4.22)

where, as in (
3.4
), the coefficients
(x_{r})_{r\geq 1}
are defined by the following Taylor series of the holomorphic differential
\phi
near the pole
z_{P}=0
of
\lambda
:

\phi(P)\underset{P\sim\infty^{0}}{=}\bigg(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}rx_{r}\mathbf{z}(P)^{r-1}\bigg)d\mathbf{z}(P),\quad\quad\text{with}\quad\mathbf{z}(P):=\lambda(P)^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}.

Note that

x_{n+1}:=\frac{1}{n+1}\underset{z_{P}=0}{{\rm res}\ }\lambda(P)dz_{P}=0

(4.23)

and
\big\{x_{n+1+\alpha}\big\}_{\alpha\geq 1}
are functions of the flat coordinates
\big\{u,x_{1},\dots,x_{n},\tau\big\}
of the flat metric
\eta(\phi)
(
2.49
), given by (
3.46
).

The general WDVV solution stated in Theorem
4.1
amounts to the following result.

Proposition 4.7

For any positive integer
n
, the WDVV solution associated with the
(n+2)
-dimensional
\phi
-Frobenius manifold structure on
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n)
and denoted by
\mathbf{F}_{\phi,n}
takes the following form:

\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\phi,n}&=\mathbf{F}_{\phi,n}(u,x_{1},\dots,x_{n},\tau)=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+\frac{u}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(2n+2+\alpha)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}x_{n+1-\alpha}\\
&\quad+\frac{n+1}{2}G_{2}(\tau)\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(n+1-\beta)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{\alpha}x_{\beta}x_{n+1-\alpha}x_{n+1-\beta}\\
&\quad+\frac{(n+1)^{2}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)}{k(n+1+\alpha)}x_{n+1-\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}\\
&\quad+\frac{(n+1)^{2}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{k=2,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n+1}\Bigg\{\left[(-1)^{k}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)}{k(n+1+\alpha)}x_{n+1-\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}\right]\\
&\quad\quad\times\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha,\ell}\frac{G_{k+\ell-1}(\tau)}{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\},\end{split}

(4.24)

where
\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}=\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{n+2-k}\big)
and
\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}=\mathcal{R}_{\alpha+k-1,k}\big(x_{1},\dots,x_{\alpha+k}\big)

are respectively the partial Bell polynomial (
2.1
) and the rational function (
2.13
). The prepotential
\mathbf{F}_{\phi,n}
(
4.24
) is a quasi-homogeneous function of degree 2 with respect to the Euler vector field

E=u\partial_{u}+\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}kx_{k}\partial_{x_{k}}

We now provide a very compact form of the WDVV solution (
4.24
) in the special cases where
n=1,2,3
.
The proof below highlights the practicality of the aforementioned formulas, including those for
x_{n+1+\alpha}
(
3.46
) and
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}
(
3.43
)-(
3.44
) which appear respectively in the Laurent series of the differential
\phi
and the elliptic function
\lambda
(
4.22
) near the pole
0
.

Corollary 4.1

We have the following WDVV solutions

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\phi,1}&=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+ux_{1}^{2}+\frac{x_{1}^{4}}{2}G_{2};\end{split}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{F}_{\phi,2}&=\frac{u^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau+3ux_{1}x_{2}-\frac{3}{4}\frac{x_{2}^{4}}{x_{1}^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}G_{2}-\frac{3}{4}x_{1}^{6}G_{4};\end{split}

(4.26)
