&\quad+\alpha\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\frac{1}{k}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n_{j}+1,k}(j)\mathcal{R}^{q}_{\alpha+k-1,k}(j)+\frac{\alpha}{n_{j}+1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\alpha-1}\frac{1}{\ell+1}\mathbf{f}_{q,\ell}(j)\mathcal{B}^{q}_{\alpha,\ell+1}(j),\end{split}

(5.35)

where
\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n,k}(j)
and
\mathcal{R}^{q}_{\mu,k}(j)
are defined (
5.8
) and
\mathbf{f}_{q,\ell}(j)
is given by (
5.33
).

Proof:
From (
5.7
), we know that

x_{q,\mu}(j)=(1-q\tau_{q})x_{\mu}(j),\quad\quad\forall\ \mu\geq 1.

This and the homogeneity properties (
2.7
) and (
2.12
) of the functions
\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}
imply that

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}(j):=\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\mu-k+1}(j)\big)=(1-q\tau_{q})^{-k}\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}^{q}(j);\\
&\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}(j):=\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}\big(x_{1}(j),\dots,x_{\mu+1}(j)\big)=(1-q\tau_{q})^{k-1}\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}^{q}(j).\end{split}

(5.36)

Therefore, we get (
5.35
) by using its non-deformed case (
3.46
), relations (
5.36
) as well as the link (
5.34
).

\square

Remark 5.1

\bullet
Let
\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi}
and
\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi_{q}}
be respectively the Bell operators (
3.7
) and (
5.8
) related to the holomorphic differentials
\phi
(
3.1
) and
\phi_{q}
(
5.3
), with
j=0,\dots,m
and
\alpha
a positive integer. Note that (
5.29
) and (
5.36
) imply that

\begin{split}\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(\cdot|\tau)\big]\Big(\frac{v}{1-q\tau_{q}}\Big)&=\sum_{r=1}^{\alpha}\frac{1}{r!}\frac{\mathcal{B}^{q}_{\alpha,r}(j)}{(1-q\tau_{q})^{r}}\mathscr{K}^{(k+r)}\Big(\frac{v}{1-q\tau_{q}}\Big|\frac{\tau_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}}\Big)\\
&=(1-q\tau_{q})^{k}\mathbf{L}_{j,\alpha}^{\phi_{q}}\big[\mathscr{K}_{q}^{(k)}(\cdot|\tau_{q})](v),\end{split}

(5.37)

where
\mathscr{K}:=\log\sigma
is the Weierstrass function and
\mathscr{K}_{q}
is its
q
-deformation (
5.28
).

\bullet
Applying formula (
2.56
), we can see that to write the
q
-analogue of the general WDVV solution
\mathbf{F}_{\phi}
from Theorem
4.1
, it suffices to replace
\phi
-flat coordinates, Eisenstein series, the Weierstrass function
\mathscr{K}(v)=\log\sigma(v)
and its derivatives, partial Bell polynomials
\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}(j)
, the rational function
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}(j)
and Bell operators
\mathbf{L}^{\phi}_{j,\alpha}\big[\mathscr{K}^{(k)}(\cdot|\tau)\big]
by their
q
-deformations listed respectively in (
5.19
), (
5.26
), (
5.28
)-(
5.29
), (
5.36
) and (
5.37
).

This will be illustrated in the next example.

Example 5.1

We shall discuss the already described WDVV solution (
5.25
) when the considered Hurwitz space is of type
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,0)
, with
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In other words, we are going to give the
q
-analogue of the WDVV prepotential
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi,n}
(
4.31
), denoted by
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},n}
.

According to the LG superpotential given by (
4.30
) and the notation used there, we deduce that the
q
-deformed flat coordinates (
5.19
)-(
5.20
) are

\displaystyle\tau_{q}:=\oint_{b}\phi_{q}=\frac{\tau}{1+q\tau};

\displaystyle x_{q,k}:=\frac{1}{k}\underset{\infty^{0}}{\rm res}\lambda(P)^{\frac{k}{n+1}}\phi_{q}(P)=\frac{x_{k}}{1+q\tau},\quad k=1,\dots,n;

\displaystyle y_{q}:=\underset{\infty^{1}}{\rm res}\lambda(P)\phi_{q}(P)=\frac{y}{1+q\tau};

\displaystyle s_{q}:=\int_{\infty^{0}}^{\infty^{1}}\phi_{q}=\frac{s}{1+q\tau};

\displaystyle\xi_{q}:=\oint_{a}\lambda(P)\phi_{q}(P)+q\oint_{b}\lambda(P)\phi_{q}(P)

\displaystyle\quad=u+\frac{{2{\rm i}\pi}q}{1+q\tau}ys+\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1+q\tau}\big(1-\delta_{n,0}\big)(n+1)\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}x_{\beta}x_{n+1-\beta}.

In particular, the inverse of the map (
5.22
) reduces to

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{-q}\Big(\tau_{q},x_{q,1},\dots,x_{q,n},y_{q},s_{q},\xi_{q}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-q\tau_{q}}\Big(\tau_{q},x_{q,1},\dots,x_{q,n},y_{q},s_{q},(1-q\tau_{q})\xi_{q}-2{{\rm i}\pi}qy_{q}s_{q}-{{\rm i}\pi}q(n+1)\mathcal{B}_{n+1,2}^{q}\Big),

with
\mathcal{B}_{n+1,2}^{q}:=\big(1-\delta_{n,0}\big)\sum_{k=1}^{n}x_{q,k}x_{q,n+1-k}
. Moreover, by (
5.31
), the
q
-deformation of the LG superpotential (
4.30
) reads as follows

\begin{split}\lambda(P)&=\xi_{q}+y_{q}s_{q}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})+\frac{n+1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,2}^{q}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})\\
&-(n+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}^{q}\big[\partial_{v}^{k}\mathscr{K}_{q}(\cdot|\tau_{q})\big]\big((1-q\tau_{q})(z_{P}-\infty^{j})\big),\end{split}

(5.38)

where
\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}^{q}=\mathcal{B}_{n+1,k}(x_{q,1},\dots,x_{q,n+2-k})
and
\mathscr{K}_{q}
is defined by (
5.28
).

On the other hand, formula (
5.25
) implies that the
q
-WDVV solution
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},n}
has the following form involving the function

\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi,n}
(
4.31
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},n}\Big(\tau_{q},x_{q,1},\dots,x_{q,n},y_{q},s_{q},\xi_{q}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi_{q}^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau_{q}+\xi_{q}y_{q}s_{q}+\frac{\xi_{q}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(2n+2+\alpha)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}

\displaystyle+(1-q\tau_{q})^{2}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi,n}\Big(\frac{\tau_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{x_{q,1}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\dots,\frac{x_{q,n}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{y_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{s_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\xi_{q}-\frac{2{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}y_{q}s_{q}-\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}(n+1)\mathcal{B}_{n+1,2}^{q}\Big)

\displaystyle-(1-q\tau)^{2}\mathbf{Q}\Big(\frac{\tau_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{x_{q,1}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\dots,\frac{x_{q,n}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{y_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\frac{s_{q}}{1-q\tau_{q}},\xi_{q}-\frac{2{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}y_{q}s_{q}-\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}(n+1)\mathcal{B}_{n+1,2}^{q}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}\big(y_{q}s_{q}\big)^{2}-\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}y_{q}s_{q}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(2n+2+\alpha)}{n+1+\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}x_{q,\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{{{\rm i}\pi}q}{1-q\tau_{q}}(n+1)\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(n+1-\beta)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,\alpha}x_{q,\beta}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\beta}.

Now, using expression (
4.31
) for
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi,n}
,
q
-Eisenstein series (
5.26
), Weierstrass function
\mathscr{K}_{q}
(
5.28
)-(
5.29
) and bearing in mind the homogeneity properties (
5.36
) of the functions
\mathcal{B}_{\mu,k}
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mu,k}
, we can conclude, after carrying out some computation, that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},n}\Big(\tau_{q},x_{q,1},\dots,x_{q,n},y_{q},s_{q},\xi_{q}\Big)=\frac{\xi_{q}^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau_{q}+\xi_{q}y_{q}s_{q}+\frac{1}{2}\xi_{q}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(2n+2+\alpha)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(y_{q}s_{q})^{2}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})+\frac{1}{2}y_{q}s_{q}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(2n+2+\alpha)}{n+1+\alpha}x_{q,\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+1}{2}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{\beta=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)(n+1-\beta)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,\alpha}x_{q,\beta}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}x_{q,n+1-\beta}

\displaystyle-y_{q}^{2}\mathscr{K}_{q}(s_{q})+\frac{n+1}{2}y_{q}\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\frac{1}{k!}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n+1,k}\mathscr{K}_{q}^{(k-1)}(-s_{q})+\frac{n+1}{2}y_{q}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)}{(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}\mathbf{L}_{\alpha}^{\phi_{q}}\big[\mathscr{K}_{q}\big](-s_{q})

\displaystyle-\frac{n+1}{2}y_{q}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\left(\frac{n+1-\alpha}{n+1+\alpha}+\frac{n+1-\alpha}{n+1}\right)x_{q,n+1-\alpha}\mathcal{R}_{\alpha,1}^{q}+\frac{(n+1)^{2}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{k=2}^{n+1}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)}{k(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n+1,k}\mathcal{R}^{q}_{\alpha+k-1,k}

\displaystyle+\frac{(n+1)^{2}}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1,k+\alpha\geq 5}^{n+1}\Bigg\{\left[(-1)^{k}\frac{(n+1-\alpha)}{k(n+1+\alpha)}x_{q,n+1-\alpha}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n+1,k}\right]\bigg[\sum_{\ell=1,k+\ell\geq 5}^{\alpha}\binom{k+\ell-1}{\ell}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{\alpha,\ell}\frac{G_{q,k+\ell-1}(\tau_{q})}{k+\ell-1}\bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+1}{2}y_{q}\sum_{\ell=4}^{n}\frac{1}{\ell(\ell+1)}\mathcal{B}^{q}_{n+1,\ell+1}G_{q,\ell}(\tau_{q})+\frac{1}{2}y_{q}^{2}\log(x_{q,1}y_{q})+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}y_{q}^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\frac{n+2}{n+1}y_{q}^{2}.

The two special cases
n=0
and
n=1
read as

\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},0}=\frac{\xi_{q}^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau_{q}+\xi_{q}y_{q}s_{q}+y_{q}^{2}\log(y_{q})+\frac{1}{2}(y_{q}s_{q})^{2}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})-y_{q}^{2}\mathscr{K}_{q}(s_{q}|\tau_{q})+\big(\log(-1)-3/2\big)y_{q}^{2};

(5.39)

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},1}&=\frac{\xi_{q}^{2}}{4{\rm i}\pi}\tau_{q}+\xi_{q}(y_{q}s_{q}+x_{q}^{2})+\frac{1}{2}y_{q}^{2}\log(x_{q}y_{q})+\frac{1}{2}\big(y_{q}^{2}s_{q}^{2}+2x_{q}^{2}y_{q}s_{q}+x_{q}^{4}\big)G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})\\
&\quad-y_{q}^{2}\mathscr{K}_{q}(s_{q}|\tau_{q})-x_{q}^{2}y_{q}\mathscr{K}_{q}^{\prime}(s_{q}|\tau_{q})+\frac{\log(-1)}{2}y_{q}^{2}-\frac{7}{4}y_{q}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.40)

Moreover, as in Theorem
4.2
, the WDVV equations for the deformed prepotential
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\phi_{q},0}
are equivalent to the following
q
-counterpart of PDEs (
4.48
)-(
4.49
):

\displaystyle\psi_{q}^{(4)}+6\big(\psi_{q}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{2}+12G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})\psi_{q}^{\prime\prime}+6\big(G_{q,2}^{2}(\tau_{q})-5G_{q,4}(\tau_{q})\big)=0;

\displaystyle(4{\rm{i}}\pi)\partial_{\tau_{q}}\psi_{q}=\psi_{q}^{\prime\prime}+\big(\psi_{q}^{\prime}\big)^{2}+3G_{q,2}(\tau_{q}),

where
\psi_{q}
is the function

\psi_{q}(v):=\mathscr{K}_{q}(v|\tau_{q})-\frac{1}{2}G_{q,2}(\tau_{q})v^{2},

and
G_{q,2}
and
\mathscr{K}_{q}
are respectively the
q
-deformed Eisenstein series (
5.26
) of weight 2 and the Weierstrass function (
5.28
).
